5b. DATA INTEGRITY CROSS-CHECK

⚠️ Bug found and fixed today: the Facebook and SVD tab reads were silently truncating recent history
`fetch_all.py` read `Facebook!A1:N2000` and `SVD!A1:O500`. Querying the spreadsheet's actual structure
today revealed the Facebook tab has follow-up columns through col AG ("8th follow up") — column N
only captures the 1st through 4th. The SVD tab similarly carries dated post-visit notes in unlabeled
columns out to col AF. Any lead dialed more than 4 times, or any post-visit lead with more than a
few follow-up notes, had its most recent contact history invisible to every report using the old
range — the truncation always cuts off the newest entries, so it systematically made recently-active
leads look stale or abandoned. Confirmed concretely: with the old range, Viren and Sandesh Padwal looked
untouched on 7 Aug (they weren't — see FOLLOW-UP above), and Jagdish Ravasia {Sushma} looked 8-9 days
neglected (he was actually contacted 3 Aug). Fixed: ranges widened to `Facebook!A1:AG2500` and
`SVD!A1:AF1200` (matching the sheets' actual `columnCount`/`rowCount` from the Sheets API, with headroom).
Re-ran the fetch and rebuilt this entire report against the corrected data. This is the same *class* of
bug as the 21-22 Jul "CRM tab enumeration" incident (a scope-limited read masquerading as a data gap) —
worth remembering that pattern generally: before reporting a lead/column/tab as "missing," check whether
the read is scoped too narrowly before concluding the team dropped the ball.
• Reverse check (facebook_tab → CRM), last 8 days (1-8 Aug, 36 rows): 36/36 = 100%.
• Reverse check, full V3 window (10 Jun–8 Aug, 290 rows): 283/290 = 97.59%. Known unresolved:

easiest fix sitting in this report.

2026-07-21

Divya Jyot LYF Rewa — Daily Snapshot — Tuesday, 21 July 2026 (CORRECTED)

⚠️ CORRECTION — read this first

The version of this report sent earlier today had a central finding that was wrong, and
Keval caught it. Full retraction and root cause below; the rest of the report is rebuilt from
corrected data.
What was wrong: the original report's headline claimed the "Divya Jyot V3 July 26 - 2BHK"
campaign was completely invisible to the CRM Event tracking — zero leads, zero speed-to-lead,
zero visit verification. That was false.
Root cause: `fetch_all.py` reads the CRM Event spreadsheet (`LEADS_SHEET_ID`) by hardcoding
`Sheet1!A1:Q5000`. What I didn't check is that this spreadsheet has two tabs: `Sheet1` is
the Studio form's feed, and **`Sheet2` is a separate, fully-working feed for the "2BHK" lead
form** — created 2026-07-06, the same day the 2BHK campaign launched, complete with exact
timestamps, phone, intent, platform, AND a real budget-qualifying question
(`what_is_your_budget_for_this_purchase?`, e.g. "below ₹1.55 cr" / "₹1.5–1.75 cr"). `fetch_all.py`
never read it, so every 2BHK lead was silently missing from `data.json`, and I built an entire
"critical tracking gap" narrative on that absence without checking for a second tab. Keval
pushed back three times (on the lead count, the CRM completeness, and specific named leads)
before this surfaced — each of his challenges was correct and this correction exists because of
that persistence, not because I caught it myself.
Fixed: `fetch_all.py` now enumerates every tab in the CRM spreadsheet automatically (see
commit) instead of hardcoding `Sheet1`, so a third form/tab in the future won't repeat this.
Everything below is rebuilt using both tabs. Note the reporting window also became a genuinely
complete day for 21 July in the process (this correction is being finished after midnight),
so funnel numbers below are slightly higher than the original partial-day (up to ~7 PM) report.

1. HEADLINE (corrected)

The 2BHK campaign is not an integrity gap — it has its own working, OTP-verified CRM feed
(Sheet2) that matches Meta's real numbers almost exactly (67 CRM leads vs. 68 reported by Meta
lifetime). With both tabs included, the Facebook-tab-to-CRM match rate for the last 30 days is
87.9% (123/140), not the 48.9% the broken version reported — much closer to the historical
93.6% baseline. The two real open items from the original report do still stand: two SVD
site visits (Vidhi Thakkar, Payal Shah) still have no CRM record in *either* tab despite a
"Meta Sent ✅" conversion pushed for them, and a live phone-typo was caught in today's own data
(Jigna Rathod: CRM has ...2833, the Facebook tab logged ...2832 — one digit off, same
shape as the Atul Thorat case from 4 July). Underneath the correction, the actual day was solid:
9 fresh leads (4 Studio + 5 "2BHK"), same-day contact on all of them, and the "2BHK" product
(confirmed via the ad creative: ₹1.55 Cr+, 2BHK, Mulund West, 5 min from MG Road station) is
real, distinct inventory.

7. ANYTHING ELSE

The two phantom-lead incidents (Hitendra Dedhia, Payal Shah) are the most important finding
this run — not because of the ad's performance (it's genuinely fine, 8.8% visit rate beats
baseline) but because someone or something is pushing conversions to Meta for site visits
that don't trace back to any real lead record. That corrupts the one signal Meta's algorithm
uses to find more people like your good leads — if a meaningful fraction of "conversions" are
phantom, the algorithm is being trained on noise, not on what a real within_3_months buyer
looks like. This is worth a direct conversation with whoever has visit-logging access, not
just a note in a report.

— exactly why CPL alone is the wrong metric here, and by the historical-baseline comparison, V3
is currently converting to visits *at or slightly above* its target rate.

past a first unanswered call. That's the single biggest recoverable pool — bigger than the

can't serve; a 30-second qualifying question on the first ring would let the team disqualify

Cross intent × speed: Of 26 `within_3_months` leads, 12 waited 2+ days and 8 waited 4+ days. These are the highest-intent people in the funnel. Being unreachable when called after 8 days is the direct result of calling 8 days too late.
What the team is doing well: 16 leads (35.6%) were contacted same-day — that's the right behavior. The problem is the 14 June-10-batch leads that appear to have been first contacted only from Jun 18 onward. It looks like a batch was set aside and worked later as a group rather than in real time.

Trend

The V3 strategy is working on quality:
• Match rate: 98% (vs. historical noise where ~400+ leads had wrong/invalid numbers)
• Site visits in 12 days: 3 (vs. ~4.5% historical over much longer windows)
• Location mismatch now a minor portion (4/45 matched = 8.9%)
Volume is lower (46 CRM-verified leads vs. 95 Meta-counted in 7 days), but quality is genuinely better. The warm lead rate of 8.9% is 4× the historical 2.3% baseline, even conservatively classified.
The main risk is speed-to-lead decay: the improvement in quality will be wasted if the team contacts leads after the OTP-window has closed.
